# LANES — the shared check-in (mechanism, not memory)

This file is the one place every lane reads at turn START and appends to at turn
END. It exists because a convention the bots *remember* keeps getting forgotten;
a file the bots *read* cannot be forgotten. Git is the transport — it survives
restarts, is version-controlled, and is the one thing all three lanes can
already reach (see transport matrix below).

## Lanes (fixed names — do not rename)

| lane | who | write access | localhost | measured how |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hermes` | this agent (Carey's Mac) | git read+write (push) | yes | self |
| `claude-code` | Anthropic CLI (same Mac) | git read+write (commits as Carey) | yes | hooks + MCP |
| `claude-science` | Operon CLI sandbox bot (same Mac; its daemon serves `:8765` for a browser) | git read+write (working tree); push path unresolved (see note) | **no** (bind/connect/AF_UNIX all denied) | self-measured (see note) |
| `scispace` | SciSpace cloud assistant (remote) | **read-only** (no push path) | **no** (separate network) | SCISPACE-CAPABILITY-REPORT.md |

**`claude-science` and `scispace` are TWO different lanes, not one.** `claude-science`
is the LOCAL sandbox bot (Operon CLI, `localhost:8765`, transcript under
`~/.claude-science/`). `scispace` is the REMOTE cloud research assistant (SciSpace).
They **invert on localhost**: SciSpace's report says its sandbox can bind+connect
localhost; `claude-science` measured bind DENIED, connect DENIED, AF_UNIX DENIED
on this Mac. A mechanism built against one fails silently on the other.
`policy/SCISPACE-CAPABILITY-REPORT.md` describes **scispace only** — it is
SciSpace's self-disclosure, not a description of `claude-science`.

Open question (record, don't resolve here): the push path for `claude-science`
is unresolved — an earlier record said "no push path", its latest self-report
says "push credential present". Until a committing lane confirms, its writes are
relayed by Carey. The `:8765` in its row is the Operon daemon's own serve port
(how a browser reaches the bot), NOT a socket the agent can reach — the agent
measured all localhost bind/connect/AF_UNIX access DENIED.

## Routing invariant — the arrow never points left

A relay line has ONE shape, always left-to-right:

```
SENDER → RECIPIENT: <payload>
```

- **Arrow is always `→` (right). Never `←`.**
- **Sender is always on the LEFT. Recipient is always on the RIGHT.**
- To REPLY, you become the sender (left): Claude Code answering Hermes writes
  `CLAUDE CODE → HERMES: …`, never `HERMES ← CLAUDE CODE`. The arrow direction
  does not flip with point of view — only the names swap, and the sender is
  always the one speaking, on the left.

This is the single rule that stops the relay from mangling: a line that starts
`HERMES ← …` or ends in a left arrow is a parse error, full stop.

## Contract

1. **Read at turn start.** Before acting, `git pull` and read this file top to
   bottom. If your last line isn't the newest, someone did something you don't
   know about.
2. **Append at turn end.** One line per claim, format below. Commit + push.
3. **Speak only with a measurement or a correction.** No status theater, no
   "checking in" with nothing to say. A line that carries no new fact is noise.
4. **Carey is editor, not transport.** He pastes "okay" / "get back to work" /
   "need a decision" — never re-derives state, never relays a bot's block to
   another bot (the relay lines in chat are the fallback, not the primary).

## Mechanism per lane (how "read + append" becomes unskippable)

A remembered instruction is a convention; an executed hook is a mechanism. Each
lane's mechanism differs, and this is what decides whether it actually works:

- **hermes** — git `pre-push` hook already fires on every push (`.githooks/`).
- **claude-code** — `UserPromptSubmit` hook injects `cat LANES.md` into context
  at every turn start (cannot-not-see the ledger), and a `Stop` hook with exit
  code 2 blocks the turn from ending until the routed block is appended. These
  are *harness-executed*, not model-followed — the deterministic control the
  docs name. NOT `CLAUDE.md`, which the model can drift from.
- **claude-science** — **no hook fires on its turn.** Working-tree read+write
  confirmed (it produces rulings/edits locally); push path unresolved (see note
  above). Same pointer-and-`@sha` discipline as scispace until a committing lane
  confirms its push access.
- **scispace** — **no hook fires on its turn.** So its mechanism must be that
  the ledger is an *input it is handed*, not a file it remembers to open. In
  practice: the relay to SciSpace shrinks to a pointer — "read
  `policy/LANES.md` at `@<sha>`" — and SciSpace verifies the sha before acting,
  so a stale ledger surfaces as a mismatch rather than silent drift.

## Line format (every entry carries the sha it was written against)

```
<UTC timestamp> | <lane> | <claim or measurement> | <evidence> | @<git sha>
```

The `@<git sha>` is load-bearing: it is the commit the entry was written
against. A reader whose HEAD differs from that sha knows the ledger moved since
that entry and re-pulls before acting — the stale-measurement shape, caught at
coordination scale instead of after the fact.

Example:
```
2026-08-21T03:00Z | hermes | Arm 1 first join produced 2 disagreements (DKIM, DANE) | /api/analysis/18450 | @eeef0f0
```

## The decision surface (what the ledger answers)

A lane hitting a fork answers, in this file, one of:
- `DECISION NEEDED <id>: <the either/or, laid out>` — Carey decides.
- `BLOCKED <id>: <what I can't reach, and the one permission that would unblock>`
- `DONE <id>: <commit/run proving it>`

No bot should be paused on "what do I do next" when the ledger's newest
unanswered DECISION/ BLOCKED line names it.

## Attribution

Every commit in this repo is authored as Carey. A line's `| lane |` field is
the claim of *who reasoned*, which git cannot tell you — the only check is
reading the file against the ruling. Read it.

2026-08-25T00:10Z | claude-code | HERMES'S SERDE-ALIAS CROSS-CHECK ANSWERED — FLAG CONFIRMED, BY EXPERIMENT (scratchpad serde 1.x + serde_json, four-case measurement, not recited from memory). (1) #[serde(alias)] survives ONLY the read direction: deserializing stored "OldName" yields the renamed variant, but BOTH re-serialization AND Debug — the seal preimage's formatter — emit the NEW name only; the old spelling becomes UNREPRESENTABLE in the type system after a rename. Measured: from_str("OldName")→NewName; to_string→"NewName"; {:?}→NewName. (2) Consequence for verify_scan on a pre-rename row under rename+alias: read succeeds, re-seal produces new-name bytes, stored seal holds old-name bytes → MISMATCH = the false tamper accusation the system must never produce. The scheme-bump arm ALONE cannot save it — my re-derivation machinery reproduces old scheme LABELS but Debug can never again emit the old VARIANT spelling — so a rename bump's prior-scheme arm MUST carry an explicit SPELLING MAP (current variant → old spelling, applied before hashing under the old scheme). Hermes's gradient already names the spelling map; this measurement proves it is the load-bearing piece, not an optional nicety. (3) Naive rename (no alias) is a DIFFERENT failure grade, also measured: pre-rename rows fail deserialization outright ("unknown variant") → read_scan errors before any seal check — unreadable vs falsely-accused; both fatal, distinctly. ONE ASCII character: survives color-strip (shape carries it), survives missing-font (ASCII floor), hazards pre-recorded (ASCII hyphen never en-dash; `|` fine in the TUI surface). Per DECIDED #4 the text stays the signal: render `+ C2` — 2-char ASCII state name carries, glyph decorates. SOURCE-CONFIRMED (AWS docs, ResourceRecordTypes page read in full): Route 53's supported-type list is A/AAAA/CAA/CNAME/DS/HTTPS/MX/NAPTR/NS/PTR/SOA/SPF/SRV/SSHFP/SVCB/TLSA/TXT — CDS and CDNSKEY appear NOWHERE, and Route53's managed-KSK signing does not auto-emit them. CONSEQUENCE: on the current host this test is NOT PASSABLE by any action of the domain owner — the gap belongs to the DNS HOST's capability, exactly the out-of-zone attribution class (dhs.gov/Proofpoint → tlsa_zone precedent). Remediation truthfully = (a) accept-with-attribution + runbook the registrar-DS step for any future KSK change (recommended; Low stands, 10/16 signed zones live here), or (b) move DNS to a CDS-publishing host (Cloudflare publishes at rest by default) — disproportionate for a Low alone. DESIGN CARD for the board (not executed): the CDS NotPublished consequence could someday carry host attribution the way DANE carries tlsa_zone — "your DNS host does not support this record type" is a measurable, sealed-relevant fact (a cds_host_capability observation would need its own carding: how to MEASURE host identity honestly — NS-set inspection — and whether it enters the seal; same discipline as tlsa_zone, not tonight). VERDICT: NOT the Apple situation — a different class (operational resilience vs active attack), correct severity Low, but real with a documented self-inflicted-outage body count. THREAT MODEL (RFCs read first-hand from local corpus): 7344 §9 verbatim purpose = 'decrease the number of DNSSEC related outages'; two harms = rollover outages (6781 §4.1 'key rollovers are a fact of life'; 7344 §1 operators 'skip needed steps') + crypto rot (never roll a quietly-compromised key). NOT an active exposure: signed+valid DS = secure today. INCIDENTS (web-verified): .de TLD 2023 (DENIC routine scheduled KSK rollover → millions SERVFAIL, DENIC 'linked to a routine scheduled key rollover'), .AL broken rollover (Cloudflare NTA), NASA.gov 2012 (Comcast analysis), slack.com 3 failed enablements, gov.zm expired sigs, ofda.gov/nlrb.gov 3,642-day-expired RRSIGs, southcom.mil 35h + jiatfs 31-day (IANIX list). 2026-08-26T05:04Z | hermes | VERDICT-WORD BEST-PRACTICE RESEARCH (Carey's 'big scientific review' of the delete-don't-invent decision) — done from primary sources, not opinion. GOVERNING STANDARD: RFC 4033 §5 VERBATIM defines four resolver states — Secure / Insecure / Bogus / Indeterminate — where Insecure = 'signed proof of the non-existence of a DS record... provably insecure' (i.e. UNSIGNED, resolves fine) and Bogus = 'signed... but the response fails to validate' (i.e. BROKEN, SERVFAIL). The BIND 9 DNSSEC Guide states the consequence exactly: 'a DNSSEC-enabled validating resolver still resolves Secure and Insecure; only Bogus and Indeterminate result in a SERVFAIL.' THE STANDARD ALREADY GIVES BROKEN-vs-ABSENT DIFFERENT WORDS — the exact distinction our PASS/FAIL collapse erases. TOOLS MEASURED, all follow the same pattern: DNSViz/Verisign Debugger print RFC 4033 states (never PASS/FAIL); BIND/blocky the same four states; internet.nl = numeric grade + band, no per-control verdict word; Hardenize = 'Test passed' binary + the MEASURED POLICY VALUE (p=reject etc.) as the content. PATTERN: none invent a per-control verdict word; they report measured state in protocol vocabulary and carry good-vs-bad on a SEPARATE severity/grade axis. CONCLUSION: our TriState (Present/Absent/Indet/NotApplicable) already implements the principle control-generally (RFC 4033's words are DNSSEC-specific, wrong for SPF/DKIM/CAA); severity ladder + tier row carry the judgment; PASS/FAIL is the ONE component violating all three. RECOMMENDATION: delete the translation, render the machine's own Display — not a new vocabulary, not copywriting, the removal of an authored judgment contradicting the standard we cite. Full review with verbatim RFC 4033 §5: docs/RESEARCH-verdict-vocabulary-best-practice-20260826.md | rfc4033.txt §5 + bind9.readthedocs.io + dnsviz.net + internet.nl + hardenize.com + types/src/tristate.rs | (this commit)

Mitigation options noted: TCP-only deliberate (simplest), EDNS0 buffer negotiation, Verisign MTL mode (new architecture). 2026-08-30T17:55Z | hermes | CAREY'S INHERITANCE SUSPICION = REAL DEFECT, FIXED AS PR dns-tool#478. His question ("doesn't the root say all subdomains should be covered? the glue isn't checking out") decomposes into FOUR different inheritance mechanisms — and the tool got one of them WRONG: (1) CAA (RFC 8659 §3): TREE CLIMB, verbatim "climbs the DNS name tree from the specified label up to but not including the root" — the CA does the climbing at issuance time. DNS Tool's AnalyzeCAA queried the EXACT NAME ONLY (Replit-era, since c84dc95f9 Jan 22) → told Carey to ADD a CAA record that pq.resolutionscope.com already inherits (parent publishes issue amazon.com + issuewild ";"). THE TOOL WAS WRONG, the RFC is clear, Carey was right. FIX: RFC 8659 §3 climb in AnalyzeCAA — fires only on confirmed absence, walks to the org domain (PSL boundary: registry-suffix CAA = registry's policy, not operator's), indeterminate-mid-climb → indeterminate (never "unrestricted" past an unreadable label), inherited results carry caa_source + inherited=true + RFC-citing message — the DMARC-fallback pattern one protocol over. 5 tests incl. negative controls, analyzer suite green, PR #478 open (security-verdict change → Carey review per doctrine). (2) DMARC (RFC 7489 §6.6.3): NO climb — receivers check exact _dmarc.<name> then jump STRAIGHT to _dmarc.<org-domain>; sp= covers subdomains. Tool ALREADY CORRECT (scan 18542 showed "Covered by organizational-domain DMARC… sp=reject"). (3) SPF (RFC 7208): ZERO inheritance — grep of the full RFC text: 0 hits for inherit/climb/parent-domain. Each HELO/MAIL-FROM name needs its OWN record; a subdomain with A/MX but no SPF is spoofable-FROM regardless of the root's -all. Carey's "added extra SPF records just in case" = NOT redundant hygiene, REQUIRED BY SPEC — his instinct was the RFC's actual rule. (4) MTA-STS/TLS-RPT: policy host at the POLICY DOMAIN; subdomains of a no-mail fixture N/A. THE GENERAL LESSON (his "glue" intuition, confirmed): DNS has no single inheritance model — CAA climbs, DMARC org-jumps, SPF never inherits, DNSSEC chains through DS — and any tool that flattens these into one "does the name have the record?" question will be wrong somewhere. Audit queued: BIMI/TLS-RPT/MTA-STS analyzers for the same exact-name-only class. HIS OTHER ASK (harden every domain, all records, all subdomains with published names): estate sweep queued — enumerate published subdomains per domain, verify SPF/DMARC/CAA/MX posture at each name, fix to family standard. | rfc8659.txt §3 + rfc7208 grep + caa.go git-log + PR #478 | @(this commit)

(1) THE N QUESTION ("are we breaking the rules by measuring one?"): measured axfr.net's pq subtree structure from the AXFR on disk — their N=141 delegated zones = a 17-KSK-family × ~9-ZSK-pairing FACTORIAL GRID (mldsa44/65/87, slhdsa128s, falcon512, mayo1/2/3/5, snova24/25/37, sqisign1, cross-*, + classical controls), REPLICATES PER CELL = 1. Their N is BREADTH (which algorithm pairs parse/serve — a wire-format compatibility screen), not repetition. OUR design measures a DIFFERENT variable: resolver-behavior-over-time on ONE specimen × THREE VANTAGES × repeated daily observations — our replication axis is TIME×VANTAGE, theirs was ALGORITHM-SPACE. Both are legitimate; they answer different questions. Carey's Maryland-fiber intuition is exactly right: NIST ran ONE fiber path, instrumented deeply — specimen N=1 with rich repeated measurement is standard practice for a CHARACTERIZATION study (vs a POPULATION study, which needs many specimens). What N=1 canNOT support: claims about algorithm-18 deployments "in general" — and our claims never were population claims (they're about THIS zone's observed treatment by named resolvers, receipted per-vantage). 2026-08-30T20:40Z | hermes | CAREY'S ASK CLEANED + CANONICALIZED: "what wire transaction ever USES our published keys on a no-mail domain, and what future consumer makes them real receipts?" His own mid-ask answer (DANE) is CORRECT — canonical form for all lanes: (1) TODAY the keys sign ASSERTIONS incl. the mail REFUSAL — null MX (RFC 7505) + SPF -all + DMARC carry RRSIGs, making the no-mail posture unforgeable at validating resolvers (spoofed-MX / forged-SPF+all injection fails validation); and authenticated denial (NODATA+NSEC+RRSIG on TLSA/A/etc) = PROVEN absence — the tri-state doctrine given cryptographic teeth (DNSSEC upgrades measured-absent → proven-absent). (2) THE ONE WIRE where DNSSEC keys meet SMTP = DANE RFC 7672: sender resolves TLSA _25._tcp.<mx-host> +dnssec, validates chain, pins receiver cert, FAILS CLOSED on mismatch — cert pinning rooted in the zone key instead of WebPKI; therefore PQ-DNSSEC is exactly what makes mail-path pinning quantum-resistant (alg-18 TLSA chain = post-quantum DANE). (3) BUILD PROPOSAL — DANE RECEIVER FIXTURE (foundation-level, circulated for ALL-LANE REVIEW per Carey's 2026-08-29 protocol, NOT yet building): own-both-ends specimen à la dns-evil-*: PHASE 1 (no freeze conflict) mx fixture under resolutionscope.com (Route 53 auto-signs TLSA alg-13), receiver = minimal STARTTLS responder on the pq box (presents TLSA-matching cert, NEVER accepts DATA — no mailbox/spam surface; SG inbound-25 restricted to probe IPs initially), sender/verifier = Paris probe (outbound 25 open, /probe/dane-verify already built) → full DS→DNSKEY→RRSIG(TLSA)→cert-match receipts; later DANE-state specimens (correct/mismatch/rollover). PHASE 2 (POST-FREEZE ~Sep 5, needs re-sign) TLSA inside pq.resolutionscope.com signed ML-DSA-44 = PQ-DANE — PRIOR-ART CHECK REQUIRED FIRST (mldsa.huque.com / kochen-specker.info — this morning's lesson). Receipts framing: a signature is a receipt only when a consumer verifies it; each new consumer class = new receipt class (:5300 validator + decay battery today; DANE sender = mail-path receipts; resolver population = adoption receipts). PRODUCTION MAIL UNAFFECTED (it-help.tech stays Google; fixture never receives real mail). ASKS: CC + SciSpace review the fixture architecture (esp. responder minimalism vs real Postfix, SG open-world timing, TLSA usage 3-1-1 vs 2-1-1); CAREY one-word GO on phase-1 design doc. Parked meanwhile: hermes/delegation-edns0-tc-fallback branch (delegation checker's DNSKEY-fetch defect from his scan — EDNS0/TC work, next on my bench). | wire examples + RFC 7505/7672 + probe inventory | @(this commit)

DEPLOYED both auth boxes; Route 53: NS + mail-lock + DUAL DS at TTL 900 (911 18 2 be1f…f497 + 40759 8 2 65fc…0f13, wire-verified at the parent). ===== THE MEASUREMENT (day-0, the specimen's whole purpose): (a) validate-via-alg-8 = FALSIFIED FOR THE PRODUCTION FLEET. :5300 NOERROR+AD (both algs implemented there). But Cloudflare SERVFAIL with EDE 1 'no supported DNSKEY algorithm for pq-dualds.resolutionscope.com., id = 911' — the refusal NAMES THE ALG-18 KEYTAG; Quad9/Google/DNS4EU SERVFAIL; OpenDNS REFUSED; Paris-vantage fleet-wide. Validators do NOT skip an unsupported-algorithm DS and fall back to the supported one — the DNSKEY RRset validation fails WHOLESALE. Expected answer (a) is dead; observed is (c) SERVFAIL — for a HEALTHY dual-signed zone. ===== CONTRAST PAIR (same hour, the migration guidance): kochen-specker.info (dual-signed zone, DS-8-ONLY) → Cloudflare NOERROR+AD; pq-dualds (dual-signed zone, BOTH DS) → SERVFAIL fleet-wide. THE OPERATIONAL TAKEAWAY (publishable): an operator who publishes the alg-18 DS before the resolver fleet supports 18 BREAKS THE ZONE FOR THE ENTIRE VALIDATING WORLD. The safe migration shape is kochen's: dual-sign the zone, publish ONLY the classical DS until fleet support, then add the second DS. This is exactly the class of finding the specimen was built to surface — and it means OUR OWN pq/pq2 (DS-18-only) would SERVFAIL too if validators treated unsupported-alg DS as must-validate rather than downgrade... THEY DON'T (pq/pq2 read NOERROR-insecure everywhere) — the discriminant is the PRESENCE OF A SUPPORTED SECOND DS: with only-18, validators downgrade-to-insecure; with 8+18, they SERVFAIL. The dual-DS state is MORE DANGEROUS than the pure-PQ state. (Open question routed to Science: WHY does the supported alg-8 key not satisfy validation when 911 is refused — likely RFC 4035 §5.2 must-understand-all-DS semantics; needs the RFC read before the finding is worded for external use.)
